\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{\textup{ad}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{+}
acts on
{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{-}
by conjugation and

\bm{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})_{+}/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})\to{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{-}\quad\text{and}\quad\bm{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})_{+}/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})\to\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{\textup{ad}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{+},

are the obvious maps, and similarly for the second definition.

By
[
26
, Lemma 4.6.4 (2)]
,
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})^{\circ}
is a subgroup of
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
but depends only on
\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{\textup{der}}
, where
\mathcal{G}^{\textup{der}}
denotes the parahoric model of
\mathbf{G}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\textup{der}}
obtained as in §
2.1.4
and
\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{\textup{der}}
is obtained from Proposition
3.14
.

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
{\mathbf{G}}
and
\mathbf{H}
be reductive groups over
\mathbb{Q}
and set
G=\mathbf{G}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
and
H=\mathbf{H}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. Fix parahoric models
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{H}
of
G
and
H
corresponding to points
x
and
y
in the buildings
\mathscr{B}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
\mathscr{B}(H,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, respectively. Suppose
f:{\mathbf{G}}\to\mathbf{H}
is a homomorphism of reductive
\mathbb{Q}
-groups carrying
\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{G}}
into
\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{H}}
, and suppose that

\displaystyle f_{\ast}:\mathscr{B}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\to\mathscr{B}(H,\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})

is a map of buildings which is equivariant for the map
f:G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\to H(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
and which sends
x
to
y
. Then
f
induces group homomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}(f)\colon\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\to\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{H}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathscr{A}(f)^{\circ}\colon\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})^{\circ}\to\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{H}})^{\circ}.

Proof.

Since
f
carries
\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{G}}
into
\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{H}}
, it follows that
f
induces a homomorphism of groups

\displaystyle f\colon{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{-}\to\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{-}.

The equivariance of
f_{\ast}
implies that
f(\operatorname{Stab}_{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})}(x))\subseteq\operatorname{Stab}_{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})}(y)
, and hence that
f(\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}))\subseteq\mathcal{H}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
. Thus
f
extends to a morphism
f:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
by
[
27
, Corollary 2.10.10]
. We similarly obtain an extension
f^{\textup{ad}}:\mathcal{G}^{\textup{ad}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\textup{ad}}
of
f^{\textup{ad}}:G^{\textup{ad}}\to H^{\textup{ad}}
, which induces

\displaystyle f^{\textup{ad}}:\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{\textup{ad}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{+}\to\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\textup{ad}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{+}.

Moreover, as
f
extends to a morphism
\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
, it follows that
f
restricts to homomorphisms

\displaystyle f^{\prime}\colon\bm{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})_{+}/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})\to\bm{\mathcal{H}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})_{+}/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}),

and

\displaystyle f^{\circ}\colon\bm{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})_{+}^{-}/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{-}\to\bm{\mathcal{H}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})_{+}^{-}/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{-}.

By Lemma
3.13
, it remains only to check that
(f,f^{\prime},f^{\textup{ad}})
and
(f^{\circ},f^{\prime},f^{\textup{ad}})
induce morphisms of tuples. By uniqueness of the extension of
G\to H\to H^{\textup{ad}}
to parahoric models and equivariance of the bijections
\mathscr{B}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathscr{B}(G^{\textup{ad}},\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
and
\mathscr{B}(H,\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathscr{B}(H^{\textup{ad}},\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
from Lemma
2.5
, we see that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})_{+}/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathcal{H}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})_{+}/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{\textup{ad}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\textup{ad}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\textup{ad}}}

commutes.
Thus it remains only to show that
f
is equivariant for the action of
\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{\textup{ad}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{+}
. But this is clear since the action of
\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{\textup{ad}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{+}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{Q})
on
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{-}
is via conjugation, and
f\colon{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{-}\to\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/\bm{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})^{-}
is a group homomorphism.
∎

3.2.4.
The group
\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})

Suppose now that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is a parahoric Shimura datum with reflex field
\mathbf{E}
. Let
{\sf K}_{p}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Fix a connected component
\mathbf{X}^{+}
of
\mathbf{X}
, which determines connected Shimura varieties
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})^{+}\subseteq\mathrm{Sh}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})^{+}\subseteq\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
.

By Lemma
3.2
, the action of
\textup{Gal}(\overline{{\mathbf{E}}}/{\mathbf{E}})
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})^{+}
factors through
\textup{Gal}({\mathbf{E}}^{p}/{\mathbf{E}})
. By abuse of notation, we will use
\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})^{+}
to refer to the
{\mathbf{E}}^{p}
-scheme obtained by descent.

Let
\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\subseteq\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\times\textup{Gal}({\mathbf{E}}^{p}/{\mathbf{E}})
denote the stabilizer of
\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})^{+}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
. By
[
26
, Lemma 4.6.6]
,
\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
is an extension of
\textup{Gal}({\mathbf{E}}^{p}/{\mathbf{E}})
by
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})^{\circ}
, and there is a canonical isomorphism

\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\ast_{\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})^{\circ}}\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\times\textup{Gal}({\mathbf{E}}^{p}/{\mathbf{E}}),

where an element of
\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
acts on
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
via conjugation by its image in
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
.
7
7

7

Explicitly, the isomorphism
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\times\textup{Gal}(\mathbf{E}^{p}/\mathbf{E})\to\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\ast_{\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})^{\circ}}\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
is given by the association
(a,\sigma)\mapsto(a\bar{e}^{-1},e)
, where
e
denotes a lift of
\sigma
to
\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
, and
\bar{e}
denotes the image of
e
in
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
.

The
\mathscr{E}
-group satisfies a functoriality akin to that of the
\mathscr{A}
-groups.

Lemma 3.16
.

Let
f:(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})\to(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
be a morphism of parahoric Shimura data. Suppose
\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mathcal{G}
correspond to points
x_{1}
and
x
in the buildings
\mathscr{B}(G_{1},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
\mathscr{B}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, respectively. Suppose the induced
f:\mathbf{G}_{1}\to\mathbf{G}
carries
\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{G}}
into
\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{H}}
, and suppose that

\displaystyle f_{\ast}\colon\mathscr{B}(G_{1},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\mathscr{B}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})

is a map of buildings which is equivariant for the map
f\colon G_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
and which sends
x_{1}
to
x
. Then
f
induces a group homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}(f):\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\to\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}).

Proof.

This follows from the definition of
\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
by Lemma
3.15
, along with the fact that
f
is a morphism of Shimura data.
∎

Suppose now we have two parahoric Shimura data
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
and
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
, and that there is a central isogeny
\alpha\colon\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{der}}\to\mathbf{G}^{\textup{der}}
which induces an isomorphism of Shimura data

\displaystyle(\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{ad}},\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\textup{ad}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}(\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ad}},\mathbf{X}^{\textup{ad}}).

Let
x_{1}
in
\mathscr{B}(G_{1},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
x
in
\mathscr{B}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denote points in the buildings of
G_{1}
and
G
, respectively, which correspond to
\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mathcal{G}
. Let us further assume that
x_{1}
and
x
correspond to the same point
x_{1}^{\textup{ad}}=x^{\textup{ad}}
in the building
\mathscr{B}(G_{1}^{\textup{ad}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\simeq\mathscr{B}(G^{\textup{ad}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Fix a connected component
\mathbf{X}_{1}^{+}
as above, which determines a group
\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})
. By the real approximation theorem, we may assume that the image of
\mathbf{X}\subseteq\mathbf{X}^{\textup{ad}}
contains
\mathbf{X}_{1}^{+}
by replacing
\mathbf{X}
by its conjugate by some element of
\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
. We set
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=\mathbf{E}_{1}\mathbf{E}
, and define
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})
to be the fiber product

(3.3)

\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\vcentcolon=\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\times_{\textup{Gal}(\mathbf{E}_{1}^{p}/\mathbf{E}_{1})}\textup{Gal}(\mathbf{E}^{\prime p}/\mathbf{E}^{\prime}).

Then
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})
is an extension of
\textup{Gal}(\mathbf{E}^{\prime p}/\mathbf{E}^{\prime})
by
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})^{\circ}
, and by
[
26
, Lemma 4.6.9]
, there is a natural map of extensions

(3.4)

\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\to\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}).

In particular, it follows that there is an isomorphism

(3.5)

\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\ast_{\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})^{\circ}}\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\times\textup{Gal}(\mathbf{E}^{\prime p}/\mathbf{E}^{\prime}).

3.3.
Construction of Kisin–Pappas–Zhou models

We now briefly recall the construction of the Kisin–Pappas–Zhou models
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
from Theorem
3.12
, focusing on the aspects most relevant for our purposes. We encourage the reader to consult the relevant parts of
[
26
]
and
[
29
]
for details.

Throughout this section we fix the following data/notation:

•

(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is a Shimura datum,

•

\mathbf{E}
is the reflex field of
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
,

•

v
is a place of
\mathbf{E}
lying over
p
,

•

\mathcal{O}_{(v)}
is the localization of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{E}}
at
v
,

•

E=\mathbf{E}_{v}
,

•

G=\mathbf{G}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
,

•

\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}_{x}^{\circ}
is a parahoric model of
G
,

•

\mathsf{K}_{p}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
,

We refer to §
2.1
and §
3.2
for the meaning of these terms, as well as other notation related to Bruhat–Tits theory and Shimura varieties.

The construction of Kisin–Pappas–Zhou models is carried out in a four step process.

Step 1: Global construction for Siegel type

Suppose that
(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})
is a Siegel datum. Choose a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattice
\Lambda\subseteq\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
and set

K_{p}=\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda)\subseteq\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}\otimes{Q}\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

For
\mathsf{K}^{p}
a neat compact open subgroups of
\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}\otimes{Q}\mathbb{A}^{f}_{p})
, we set
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}{\sf K}^{p}}(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})
to be the
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-scheme given by Mumford’s moduli of principally polarized abelian varieties with
\mathsf{K}^{p}
-level structure (see
[
8
, §4]
). These form a
\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}\otimes{Q}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-system of
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-schemes.

Step 2: Construction for Hodge type data

Let us now assume that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is a parahoric Hodge type datum and let us fix a parahoric Hodge embedding

\iota\colon(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})\to(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm},\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda)),

which respects stabilizers. As in
[
22
, Lemma 2.10]
we may choose a neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{GSp}(\mathbf{V}\otimes{Q}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that
\iota(\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p})\subseteq\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda)\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}
, and such that the map

(3.6)

\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda)\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}}(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})_{\mathbf{E}},

is a closed embedding.

We then set

•

\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
to be the normalization of the Zariski closure of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
in the scheme
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda)\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}}(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{O}_{(v)}}
, via the embedding in (
3.6
),

•

\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
to be the normalization (in the sense of
[
47
,
Tag 0BAK
]
) of
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
relative to finite map

\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X}).

Remark 3.17
.

As each
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda)\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}}(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{O}_{(v)}}
is quasi-projective over
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
(see
[
34
, Theorem 7.9]
), Zariski closed embeddings are quasi-projective, normalization maps are finite in our setting (see
[
47
,
Tag 035R
]
and
[
47
,
Tag 07QW
]
) and thus quasi-projective, we deduce that each
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is a quasi-projective
\mathcal{O}_{(v)}
-scheme.

As Zariski closure and normalization are functorial constructions, we see that
\{\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
form a projective system. We then further set

\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\vcentcolon=\varprojlim_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X}).

The continuous
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-action of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E}
extends to a continuous action on
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
with the property that

\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})/\mathsf{K}^{p}\simeq\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X}),

compatibly in
\mathsf{K}^{p}
. We finally set

\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\vcentcolon=\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}\ \text{ and }\ \mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\vcentcolon=\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{v}},

which is a scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{v}
with
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-action and a scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{v}
, respectively.

A priori, these schemes depend on the choice of parahoric Hodge embedding which respects stabilizers. That said, the following shows that the not case, thus justifying the omission of the parahoric Hodge embedding from the notation.

Proposition 3.18

(
[
29
, Corollary 5.3.3]
,
[
42
, Theorem 4.5.2]
, and
[
10
]
)
.

The objects
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
,
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
,
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
, and
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
are canonically independent of parahoric Hodge embedding.

While the action of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
extends to actions on
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
and
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
, it is not a priori clear that these can be extended further to actions of
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
. That said, this does hold true when
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is very good, as explained in
[
26
, §4.6]
.
8
8

8

Indeed, the fact that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is very good implies, in particular, that
Z(\mathbf{G})
is an
R
-smooth torus, and so Proposition
2.12
applies. This means that there is a group map
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\to\mathscr{B}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{x})
, where, if
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}_{x}^{\circ}
, the group scheme
\mathcal{G}_{x}
is the stabilizer Bruhat–Tits group scheme as in
[
26
, §1.1.2]
, and
\mathscr{B}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{x})
is as in
[
26
, §4.5.6]
. Then, one can define the action precisely as in
[
26
, Lemma 4.5.7]
.

Step 3: Global construction relative to a very good Hodge type datum

Suppose now that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is a general acceptable parahoric Shimura datum of abelian type. The key proposition which allows us to bootstrap from the previous cases is the following.

Proposition 3.19

(
[
29
, Proposition 5.2.6]
)
.

There exists a very good parahoric Shimura datum
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
of Hodge type well-adapted to
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
.

Proof.

The only claim that needs to be checked is that
G^{\textup{der}}
may be taken to be
R
-smooth. But this follows by the construction in the proof of loc. cit. (cf.
[
29
, §5.3.10]
).
∎

Throughout the remainder of this section, we fix
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
as in the proposition above. Choose connected components
\mathbf{X}^{+}\subseteq\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{X}_{1}^{+}\subseteq\mathbf{X}_{1}
which correspond in
\mathbf{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}\simeq\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\mathrm{ad}}
. Using Lemma
3.2
, we obtain connected components
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})^{+}
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})^{+}
defined over
\mathbf{E}^{p}
and
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{p}
, respectively. We also obtain subgroups

\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\subseteq\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\times\textup{Gal}(\mathbf{E}^{p}/\mathbf{E}),\qquad\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\subseteq\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\times\textup{Gal}(\mathbf{E}_{1}^{p}/\mathbf{E}),

and the group

\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})=\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\times_{\textup{Gal}(\mathbf{E}_{1}^{p}/\bm{E})}\textup{Gal}(\mathbf{E}^{\prime p}/\bm{E}),

as defined in §
3.2
.

We have a bijection

(3.7)

\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})_{\bm{E}_{1}^{p}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\pi_{0}(\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{p}}}),

as follows from combining the following general results.

Lemma 3.20
.

Let
\{S_{i}\}
be a projective system of spectral spaces with quasi-compact surjective transition maps. Write
S=\varprojlim S_{i}
. Then,

(1)

S
is a spectral space,

(2)

the
(
dense
)
quasi-compact open subsets of
S
are precisely those of the form
U=\varprojlim U_{i}
with each
U_{i}\subseteq S_{i}
a
(
dense
)
quasi-compact open subset such that
U_{i}
is the preimage of
U_{j}
under
S_{i}\to S_{j}
,

(3)

the natural map
\pi_{0}(S)\to\pi_{0}(S_{i})
is surjective for all
i
,

(4)

the natural map
\pi_{0}(S)\to\lim\pi_{0}(S_{i})
is a homeomorphism.

Moreover, if each
S_{i}
is a disjoint union of finitely many irreducible spaces, then for every dense quasi-compact open
U\subseteq S
the natural map
\pi_{0}(U)\to\pi_{0}(S)
is a homeomorphism.

Proof.

Claim (1) follows from
[
11
, Chapter 0, Theorem 2.2.10]
. Claim (2) follows from
[
11
, Chapter 0, Proposition 2.2.9 and Lemma 2.2.19]
. Claim (4) follows from
[
1
, Lemma 4.1.6]
), and claim (3) then follows from claim (4), as each projection
\pi_{0}(S_{i})\to\pi_{0}(S_{j})
is a surjection of compact Hausdorff spaces (see
[
47
,
Tag 0906
]
), and so
\varprojlim\pi_{0}(S_{i})\to\pi_{0}(S_{i})
is surjective by Tychonoff’s theorem. For the final claim write
U=\varprojlim U_{i}
with
U_{i}\subseteq S_{i}
a dense quasi-compact open. Then, each
\pi_{0}(U_{i})\to\pi_{0}(S_{i})
is clearly a bijection, and so the claim follows by passing to the limit using (4).
∎

Lemma 3.21
.

Let
X\to Y
be a flat and finite type morphism of Noetherian schemes where
X
is normal. Then,
X
is a finite disjoint union of irreducible spaces, and for any dense open
U\subseteq Y
, the open
X_{U}\subseteq X
is dense.

Proof.

The first claim follows from
[
47
,
Tag 033N
]
, and the latter is clear as
f
is open by
[
47
,
Tag 01UA
]
.
∎

The bijection (
3.7
) shows that
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})^{+}
corresponds uniquely to a connected component
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})^{+}
of
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})
. Set
\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime p}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{E}}}\mathcal{O}_{(v)}
. Consider then

(3.8)

\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})^{+}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}\times\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}).

As observed in
Step 2
, we have that the right
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})
-action extends to
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})
and by the bijection (
3.7
) we deduce that
\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})
stabilizes
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})^{+}
. We then get an induced action of
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})
on
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})^{+}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}
. We then finally let
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})
act on the right of (
3.8
) by setting

(3.9)

(s,a)\cdot e=(se,\overline{e}^{-1}a\overline{e}),

where
\overline{e}
denotes the image of
e
under the homomorphisms

(3.10)

\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\to\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\to\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}),

where the first map is as in (
3.4
), and the second map is projection.

We then consider
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\rtimes\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})
, where
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})
acts again via the homomorphism from (
3.10
). Let the semi-direct product
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\rtimes\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})
act on (
3.8
) by having
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
act by right multiplication on the
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
-factor of (
3.8
), i.e.,

(3.11)

(s,a)\cdot a^{\prime}=(s,aa^{\prime}).

As in (
3.2
) we obtain an action of
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\ast_{\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})^{\circ}}\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})
on

(3.12)

\left[\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}^{{{\varnothing}}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})^{+}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}\times\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\right]/\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})^{\circ},

with notation as in loc. cit. Since

\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\ast_{\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})^{\circ}}\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\simeq\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\times\textup{Gal}(\mathbf{E}^{\prime p}/\mathbf{E}),

(see (
3.5
)), we obtain an action of
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\times\textup{Gal}(\mathbf{E}^{\prime p}/\mathbf{E})
, and hence of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times\textup{Gal}(\mathbf{E}^{\prime p}/\mathbf{E})
, on (
3.12
).

Now, let
J
denote the image of the natural map

\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\to\mathscr{A}(\mathbf{G}_{1})^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{A}(\mathbf{G})/K_{p},

where the map
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})^{\circ}\to\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
is as in Lemma
3.15
. Then
J
is finite. Let
\mathfrak{d}=(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
, and consider

\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}\vcentcolon=\left([\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}^{+}\times\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})]/\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})^{\circ}\right)^{J},

which we endow with the diagonal (relative to the
J
-indexed factors) action of the group
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times\textup{Gal}(\mathbf{E}^{\prime p}/\mathbf{E})
. This action is continuous, and so by Galois descent gives to a unique scheme
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{(v)}^{\prime}}
with continuous action of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, where
\mathcal{O}_{(v)}^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{E}}}\mathcal{O}_{(v)}
.
9
9

9

That Galois descent applies here follows from the continuity of the action, the quasi-projectivity observation made in Remark
3.17
, and the fact that quasi-projective morphisms satisfy effective descent relative to finite locally free maps (see
[
47
,
Tag 0CCJ
]
).

Step 4: The construction in the local setting

Consider

(\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}.

That
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
is completely split over
\mathbf{E}
at every prime over
p
implies, in particular, that this decomposes as several copies of the same
\mathcal{O}_{v}
-scheme with a continuous action of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, indexed by by the place of
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
lying over
v
. Choose any of these copies, and call it
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
. We then set

\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\vcentcolon=\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})/\mathsf{K}^{p},

for any neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}^{p}
of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. That these quotients exist and are quasi-projective can be deduced from Remark
3.17
in conjunction with
[
47
,
Tag 01ZY
]
and
[
47
,
Tag 07S6
]
.

3.4.
Some properties and functoriality results

In this subsection we establish some compatibilities satisfied by the Kisin–Pappas–Zhou integral models, and we prove the minimal amount of functoriality needed to prove our main result (see Theorem
4.10
). This main result will then establish functoriality in general (see Theorem
4.14
).

3.4.1.
Kisin–Pappas–Zhou integral models of toral type

To utilize the results of
[
7
]
, it is useful to explicitly describe Kisin–Pappas–Zhou integral models of tori.

Recall from Example
3.7
that a Shimura datum
(\mathbf{T},\{h\})
is of toral type if
\mathbf{T}
is a torus. In this case, for every compact open subgroup
{\sf K}={\sf K}_{p}{\sf K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, the Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}}(\mathbf{T},\{h\})
is zero-dimensional, i.e.,

(3.13)

\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}}(\mathbf{T},\{h\})_{E}=\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\textup{Spec}\,(E_{i})

for some finite index set
I
. In fact, each field
E_{i}
is the same field, and is an unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
(see
[
7
, Lemma 4.2]
and the proof of
[
7
, Lemma 4.8]
).

Set
T=\mathbf{T}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. As discussed in §
2.1.3
, there is a unique parahoric model
\mathcal{T}
of
T
, given by the connected component
(\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{lft}})^{\circ}
of the Néron model
\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{lft}}
of
T
.

Proposition 3.22
.

Suppose
(\mathbf{T},\{h\},\mathcal{T})
is parahoric Shimura datum of toral type, and let
\mathfrak{d}=(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
be a very good parahoric Shimura datum of Hodge type which is well-adapted to
(\mathbf{T},\{h\},\mathcal{T})
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{\sf K}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{T},\{h\})=\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\textup{Spec}\,(\mathcal{O}_{E_{i}}),

for any neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and with
{\sf K}={\sf K}_{p}{\sf K}^{p}
. The same holds true for
\mathscr{S}_{\sf K}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{T},\{h\})
if
(\mathbf{T},\{h\})
is of Hodge type.

Proof.

First, observe that as
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})
is adapted to the datum
(\mathbf{T},\{h\})
, we have an isogeny
\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{der}}\to\mathbf{T}^{\textup{der}}=\{e\}
, and hence
\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{der}}
is trivial, since it is connected. Thus,
\mathbf{G}_{1}
is a torus.
10
10

10

Indeed, if
H
is a commutative reductive group over a field
K
, and
T
is a maximal torus of
H
, then
H=Z_{H}(T)=T
(see
[
37
, Corollary 17.84]
).
Consider an arbitrary neat compact open subgroup
{\sf K}^{p}_{1}\subseteq\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.

Choose a parahoric Hodge embedding
\iota\colon(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})\to(\mathbf{GSp}(\mathbf{V}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm},\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda))
which respects stabilizers, and a neat compact open subgroup
{\sf L}_{1}^{p}\subseteq\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}\otimes{Q}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that
\iota({\sf K}_{1,p}\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1})\subseteq\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda){\sf L}_{1}^{p}
, inducing a closed embedding

(3.14)

\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}_{1,p}\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda){\sf L}_{1}^{p}}(\mathbf{GSp}(\mathbf{V}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}.

Note that
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda){\sf L}_{1}^{p}}(\mathbf{GSp}(\mathbf{V}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})
is flat, separated, and finite over
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
. Thus, considering (
3.13
), and using notation from
Step 2
of §
3.3
, it follows from Lemma
3.23
below that
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}^{{\varnothing}}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}_{p,1}{\sf K}^{p}_{1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})
and thus
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}{\sf K}^{p}_{1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})
are disjoint unions of semi-local localizations of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{F}}
for some finite extension
\mathbf{F}
of
\mathbf{E}
.

This implies that, using notation now from
Step 3
of §
3.3
, that
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{+}
is either the spectrum of a field or of a semi-local Dedekind domain. Thus, from the construction in
Step 4
of §
3.3
we deduce that for each neat compact open
\mathsf{K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
one has that
\mathscr{S}_{{\sf K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is either the disjoint union of spectra of extensions of
E
, or the disjoint union of spectra of the rings of integers of finite extensions of
E
. That said, the former is impossible as it is then clear that such a model cannot satisfy the extension property from (2) of Theorem
3.12
, and so the latter must hold. The fist claim follows. Moreover, the second claim follows from similar arguments.

∎

Following
[
47
,
Tag 035E
]
, if
X
is a scheme with the property that every quasi-compact open has finitely many connected components, then we denote the normalization of
X
by
X^{\nu}
.

Lemma 3.23
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
be an excellent Dedekind domain and
\mathscr{X}
be a separated, finite type, flat
\mathcal{O}
-scheme. Let
x
is a closed point of
\mathscr{X}_{\eta}
and let
\overline{x}
be the Zariski closure of
x
in
\mathscr{X}
. Then,
\overline{x}^{\nu}
is an open subscheme of
\textup{Spec}\,(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
for a Dedekind domain
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
finite flat over
\mathcal{O}
.

Proof.

Let
\overline{X}
be a compactification of
\mathscr{X}\to\textup{Spec}\,(\mathcal{O})
(see
[
47
,
Tag 0ATT
]
and
[
47
,
Tag 0F41
]
). Replacing
\overline{\mathscr{X}}
with the closed suscheme cut out by the
\mathcal{O}
-torsion in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}
, we may assume that
\overline{\mathscr{X}}
is flat over
\mathcal{O}
(see
[
17
, Proposition 14.14]
).

Let
Z
denote the Zariski closure of
x
in
\overline{\mathscr{X}}
. We claim that
Z^{\nu}=\textup{Spec}\,(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
for a Dedekind ring
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
finite flat over
\mathcal{O}
. As
\overline{x}=\mathscr{X}\cap Z
, the lemma will then follow from
[
47
,
Tag 035K
]
. To prove the claim, we observe that
Z
is evidently irreducible and reduced, and thus integral. But, as
Z\to\mathrm{Spec}(\mathcal{O})
is dominant, we deduce that it is flat by
[
17
, Proposition 14.14]
. But, since
\overline{\mathscr{X}}
is a proper
\mathcal{O}
-scheme, so is
Z
. Thus, by
[
47
,
Tag 0D4J
]
we deduce that
Z_{s}
is zero dimensional. Thus,
Z\to\textup{Spec}\,(\mathcal{O})
is proper and quasi-finite, and so finite (see
[
47
,
Tag 02OG
]
). It follows that
Z^{\nu}
is also an integral scheme and
Z^{\nu}\to\textup{Spec}\,(\mathcal{O})
is dominant and finite (using
[
47
,
Tag 035R
]
) over
\mathcal{O}
and so flat again by
[
17
, Proposition 14.14]
. Write
Z^{\nu}=\textup{Spec}\,(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
, then
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
is
1
-dimensional Noetherian normal domain, and so a Dedekind domain as desired.
∎

Given Proposition
3.22
, we see that the definition of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathfrak{d}}=(\mathbf{T},\{h\})
is independent of the choice of
\mathfrak{d}
. We denote by
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{T},\{h\})
the common object.

3.4.2.
Functoriality for ad-isomorphisms

Suppose that
\alpha\colon(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})\to(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is an ad-isomorphism of parahoric Shimura datum. Take a very good parahoric Shimura datum
\mathfrak{d}=(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathcal{G}_{2})
of Hodge type well-adapted to
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
. Then, since
\alpha
is an ad-isomorphism, one sees that
\mathfrak{d}
is also well-adapted to
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
in the obvious way.

Proposition 3.24
.

There exists a unique quotient-finite étale morphism

\alpha\colon\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}},

which is equivariant for the map
\alpha\colon\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and models

\alpha\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E_{1}}.

Here by
quotient-finite étale
we mean that if
\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
\mathsf{K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
are neat compact open subgroups such that
\alpha(\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p})\subseteq\mathsf{K}^{p}
, then the induced map

\alpha\colon\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})\to\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}},

is a finite étale morphism.

Proof of Proposition
3.24
.

The fact that such a map is unique is clear by the separatedness of
\alpha
and the flatness of
\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})
, which guarantees that its generic fiber is Zariski dense (e.g., see
[
17
, Proposition 14.14]
).

To see the existence, we note that by definition we have

\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})=\left(\left[\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,2}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2})^{+}_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}\times\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\right]/\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{2})^{\circ}\right)^{J_{1}},

and

\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}=\left(\left[\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,2}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2})^{+}_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}\times\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\right]/\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{2})^{\circ}\right)^{J}.

Now, by Lemma
3.15
and Lemma
3.16
we get morphisms

\alpha\colon\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\to\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}),\quad\alpha\colon\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})^{\circ}\to\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}),\ \text{ and }\ \alpha\colon\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\to\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}).

Because
\alpha(\mathsf{K}_{p,1})\subseteq\mathsf{K}_{p}
, we have a map of sets
\alpha\colon J_{1}\to J
. Since the isomorphism from (
3.5
) is natural in these operations, we deduce the existence of a map

\left(\left[\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,2}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2})^{+}_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}\times\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\right]/\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{2})^{\circ}\right)^{J_{1}}\to\left(\left[\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,2}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2})^{+}_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}\times\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\right]/\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{2})^{\circ}\right)^{J},

equivariant for
\alpha\colon\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. That the generic fiber of this map agrees with

\alpha\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})_{E_{1}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E_{1}}.

follows as in
[
26
, Lemma 4.6.13]
.

Finally, we show that
\alpha
is quotient-finite étale. Let
\mathscr{S}_{1}^{+}
denote a connected component of
\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{{\sf K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})
mapping to a connected component
\mathscr{S}^{+}
of
\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}
. By construction, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{2}^{+}=\mathscr{S}^{{\varnothing}}_{{\sf K}_{p,2}}(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2})^{+}_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}/\Delta_{1}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathscr{S}^{+}=\mathscr{S}^{{\varnothing}}_{{\sf K}_{p,2}}(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2})^{+}_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}/\Delta,

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}=\ker(\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{2})^{\circ}\to\mathscr{A}(\mathbf{\bm{\mathcal{G}}}))\quad\text{and}\quad\Delta=\ker(\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{2})^{\circ}\to\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})),

(see also the arguments of
[
26
, Corollary 4.6.15]
). Thus
\mathscr{S}^{+}
is the quotient of
\mathscr{S}_{1}^{+}
by the finite group
\Delta/\Delta_{1}
, and the result follows.
∎

In particular, the proposition implies functoriality for morphisms
(\mathbf{T}_{1},\{h_{1}\},\mathcal{T}_{1})\to(\mathbf{T},\{h\},\mathcal{T})
of parahoric Shimura data of toral type.

3.4.3.
Functoriality for abelianizations

Suppose now that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is a parahoric Shimura datum of abelian type. Consider the parahoric Shimura datum
(\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}},\mathbf{X}^{\textup{ab}},\mathcal{G}^{\textup{ab}})
, where
\mathbf{X}^{\textup{ab}}
is the result of post-composing any element
h
of
\mathbf{X}
with the canonical map
\delta:\mathbf{G}\to\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}}
. We have the obvious associated morphism of parahoric Shimura data

\alpha\colon(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})\to(\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}},\mathbf{X}^{\textup{ab}},\mathcal{G}^{\textup{ab}}).

Write
{\sf K}_{p}^{\textup{ab}}=\mathcal{G}^{\textup{ab}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Proposition 3.25
.

There exists a unique

\alpha\colon\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}^{\textup{ab}}}(\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}},\mathbf{X}^{\textup{ab}})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}},

which is equivariant for the map
\alpha\colon\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and models

\alpha\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})_{E_{1}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E_{1}}.

Proof.

Given the descriptions of
\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}_{p}^{\textup{ab}}}(\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}},\mathbf{X}^{\textup{ab}})
and
\mathscr{S}_{{\sf K}_{p}^{\textup{ab}}}(\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}},\mathbf{X}^{\textup{ab}})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
from (
3.13
) and Proposition
3.22
, respectively, the result follows from Lemma
3.26
. More precisely, it suffices to construct maps at every finite level. But, by Proposition
3.22
, the Kisin–Pappas–Zhou model
S^{\prime}
for
(\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}},\mathbf{X}^{\textup{ab}})
base-changed to
\mathcal{O}_{E}
is finite flat over
S=\textup{Spec}\,(\mathcal{O}_{E})
. On the other hand, the finite levels
X
of the Kisin–Pappas–Zhou models
X
for
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
are normal and quasi-projective and flat over
S
. Thus may apply Lemma
3.26
to the map at finite levels on the generic fiber.
∎

Lemma 3.26
.

Suppose that
S
is a scheme and
U
is an open subscheme of
S
which is schematically dense and for which the inclusion
U\hookrightarrow S
is quasi-compact. Given a diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

with
X
a normal scheme,
f
quasi-compact, separated, and flat, and
g
quasi-compact, flat, and integral, there exists a unique arrow dashed arrow as in the diagram above which makes the top square Cartesian.

Proof.

By
[
47
,
Tag 081H
]
,
X_{U}\subseteq X
is schematically dense, so the uniqueness follows from
[
47
,
Tag 01RH
]
. In turn, by uniqueness, we may localize on
S
and
X
to prove existence. Using that
g
is affine, we may then assume without loss of generality that
S=\textup{Spec}\,(A)
,
S^{\prime}=\textup{Spec}\,(A^{\prime})
and
A\to A^{\prime}
is integral. Moreover, localizing on
X
we may further assume
X=\textup{Spec}\,(B)
with
B
a normal domain. We then have the following diagram of rings
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(S_{U}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(X_{U})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

The center and right-hand vertical arrows are injections by flatness of
f
and
g
along with
[
47
,
Tag 081H
]
and
[
47
,
Tag 01RE
]
.

We claim that
\alpha(A^{\prime})\subseteq B
. Indeed, let
a^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}
. Then by integrality of
A\to A^{\prime}
, there exists a monic polynomial
p(T)
in
A[T]
such that
p(a^{\prime})=0
. But then
\alpha(a^{\prime})
in
\mathcal{O}(X_{U})
satisfies the monic polynomial
f(p)(T)
in
B[T]
. Thus
\alpha(a^{\prime})
must lie in
B
by normality of
B
.

It follows that we have a map
\textup{Spec}\,(\alpha):\textup{Spec}\,(B)\to\textup{Spec}\,(A^{\prime})
. We claim that the diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X=\textup{Spec}\,(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}=\textup{Spec}\,(A^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Spec}\,(\alpha)}

is Cartesian. For this it is enough to show that
\textup{Spec}\,(\alpha)\big|_{X_{U}}=\alpha
. Since
U\hookrightarrow X
is quasi-compact,
U
is quasi-compact, and therefore the same is true of
S_{U}^{\prime}
and
X_{U}
by quasi-compactness of
f
and
g
. Since
\textup{Spec}\,(\alpha)
and
\alpha
induce the same map
\mathcal{O}(S^{\prime}_{U})\to\mathcal{O}(X_{U})
, the result now follows from
[
47
,
Tag 01P9
]
.
∎

3.4.4.
Two constructions for very good Hodge-type data

We record here the following basic compatibility between the constructions made in §
3.3
. Let
\mathfrak{d}=(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
be a very good parahoric Shimura datum of Hodge type.

Proposition 3.27
.

There is a canonical
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})
-equivariant identification

\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathscr{S}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}},(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X}),

extending the identity in the generic fiber.

Proof.

We first construct a
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant map

\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}\to\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}.

Choosing a connected component
\mathbf{X}^{+}
of
\mathbf{X}
, we obtain an inclusion

\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}^{+}\hookrightarrow\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}},

which extends by the action of
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
to

(3.15)

\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}^{+}\times\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\to\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}

via
(s,a)\mapsto s\cdot a
. One checks that (
3.15
) is equivariant for the action of
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\rtimes\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
on
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}^{+}\times\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
, and it induces

(3.16)

[\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}^{+}\times\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})]/\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})^{\circ}\to\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}.

Indeed, this follows by the definition of the action of
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\rtimes\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
on the product
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}^{+}\times\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
via (
3.9
) and (
3.11
), along with the fact that the composition

\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})^{\circ}\to\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\to\mathscr{E}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\to\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})

is the inclusion
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})^{\circ}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
. In turn, (
3.16
) determines a
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})\ast_{\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})^{\circ}}\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
-equivariant, and hence
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\times\textup{Gal}(\mathbf{E}^{\prime p}/\mathbf{E})
-equivariant, map

(3.17)

\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}=\left([\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}^{+}\times\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})]/\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})^{\circ}\right)^{J}\to\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}.

The map (
3.17
) is birational, since it is an isomorphism on generic fibers by
[
26
, Lemma 4.6.13]
, and, by its definition, it is quasi-finite. By
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariance, we may first pass to the map (
3.17
) after taking the quotient by any neat compact open subgroup
{\sf K}^{p}
of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. In that case, the target is normal, so it follows that the quotient of (
3.17
) by
{\sf K}^{p}
is an open immersion on connected components by Lemma
3.21
and
[
17
, Corollary 12.88]
. Passing to the limit, we deduce that (
3.17
) is an open immersion. Moreover, (
3.17
) is surjective by part (2) of Theorem
3.12
11
11

11

Indeed, it is enough to show (
3.17
) is surjective on special fibers. An
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-point of
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}
factors through an
R
-point of
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}
, where
R
is a discrete valuation ring of characteristic
(0,p)
(see
[
47
,
Tag 0CM2
]
). This, in turn, induces an
R[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
-point of
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}
. We can pull this point back along the isomorphism on generic fibers, and the result lifts to an
R
-point of
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}
by part (2) of Theorem
3.12
. This maps to the original
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-point of
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{S}}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime p}_{(v)}}
by separatedness, as they generically agree.
, so it is an isomorphism.

∎

3.4.5.
Kisin–Pappas–Zhou integral models for some products

Let
\mathfrak{d}=(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
be a very good parahoric Shimura datum of Hodge type, and that
(\mathbf{T},\{h\},\mathcal{T})
is a parahoric Shimura datum of toral type. Write
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
for the compositum of the reflex fields of the two Shimura data, which is the reflex field of
(\mathbf{G}\times\mathbf{T},\mathbf{X}\times\{h\})
, and set
\mathsf{M}_{p}=\mathcal{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Proposition 3.28
.

There is a canonical
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})\times\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant identification

\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{{\sf K}_{p}\times\mathsf{M}_{p}}(\mathbf{G}\times\mathbf{T},\mathbf{X}\times\{h\})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathscr{S}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}}}\times\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{M}_{p}}(\mathbf{T},\{h\})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}}}.

Proof.

The projection map
\mathbf{G}\times\mathbf{T}\to\mathbf{G}
is an ad-isomorphism, so by Proposition
3.24
, we have a quotient-finite map

(3.18)

\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{{\sf K}_{p}\times\mathsf{M}_{p}}(\mathbf{G}\times\mathbf{T},\mathbf{X}\times\{h\})\to\mathscr{S}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}}}

extending the morphism on the generic fibers which is equivariant for the projection
(\mathbf{G}\times\mathbf{T})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. On the other hand, the map of reductive groups
\mathbf{G}\times\mathbf{T}\to\mathbf{T}
factors through
(\mathbf{G}\times\mathbf{T})^{\textup{ab}}
, which is a torus. Hence Proposition
3.24
and Proposition
3.25
combine to furnish us with a map

\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{{\sf K}_{p}\times\mathsf{M}_{p}}(\mathbf{G}\times\mathbf{T},\mathbf{X}\times\{h\})\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{M}_{p}}(\mathbf{T},\{h\})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}}}

extending the morphism on generic fibers, which is equivariant for
(\mathbf{G}\times\mathbf{T})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. From these, we obtain the map

(3.19)

\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{{\sf K}_{p}\times\mathsf{M}_{p}}(\mathbf{G}\times\mathbf{T},\mathbf{X}\times\{h\})\to\mathscr{S}_{{\sf K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}}}\times\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{M}_{p}}(\mathbf{T},\{h\})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}}},

which is equivariant for
(\mathbf{G}\times\mathbf{T})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. The map (
3.19
) is an isomorphism on generic fibers, and is quasi-finite at each finite level because (
3.18
) is quotient-finite. It follows that (
3.19
) is an isomorphism by the argument at the end of the proof of Proposition
3.27
.
∎

4.
The Pappas–Rapoport conjecture

In this section we state the Pappas–Rapoport conjecture and recall the cases in which it has been previously proven. We also state our main theorem and applications.

As in §
3.3
, throughout this section we often use the following notation and data:

•

(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is a Shimura datum,

•

\mathbf{Z}
is the center of
\mathbf{G}
,

•

\mathbf{E}
is the reflex field of
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
,

•

v
is a
p
-adic place of
\mathbf{E}
,

•

E=\mathbf{E}_{v}
,

•

k_{E}
is the residue field of
E
,

•

G=\mathbf{G}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
,

•

\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}_{x}^{\circ}
is a parahoric model of
G
,

•

\mathsf{K}_{p}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
,

•

{\sf K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}^{f}_{p})
is a neat compact open subgroup,

•

{\sf K}={\sf K}_{p}{\sf K}^{p}
.

4.1.
The Pappas–Rapoport conjecture

We now recall the formulation of the Pappas–Rapoport conjecture as given in
[
42
]
and extended in
[
7
]
.

4.1.1.
The group
\mathcal{G}^{c}

In constrast to the case of Shimura varieties of Hodge type discussed in
[
42
]
, to obtain shtukas on arbitrary Shimura varieties of abelian type, one must consider a modification
\mathcal{G}^{c}
of the parahoric group
\mathcal{G}
.

Let
\mathbf{T}
be a multiplicative group over
\mathbb{Q}
. We denote by
\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{ac}}
the anti-cuspidal part of
\mathbf{T}
as in
[
28
, Definition 1.5.4]
. More precisely, if
\mathbf{T}_{a}
denotes the largest anisotropic subtorus of
\mathbf{T}
, then
\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{ac}}
is the largest subtorus of
\mathbf{T}_{a}
whose base change to
\mathbb{R}
contains the maximal split subtorus of
(\mathbf{T}_{a}){R}
.

We write
{\mathbf{G}}^{c}
for the quotient
{\mathbf{G}}^{c}/\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{ac}}
, and if
\mathsf{H}
is a subgroup of
{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, we write
\mathsf{H}^{c}
for the image of
\mathsf{H}
under
{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to{\mathbf{G}}^{c}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. We also denote by
G^{c}
the base change of
{\mathbf{G}}^{c}
to
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. The following lemma will be used below.

Lemma 4.1
.

If
\alpha\colon(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})\to(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is a morphism of Shimura data then
\alpha(\mathbf{Z}_{1,\mathrm{ac}})\subseteq\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{ac}}
and so
\alpha
induces a map
\alpha\colon\mathbf{G}_{1}^{c}\to\mathbf{G}^{c}
. Consequently, if
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is of Hodge type, then the natural maps
\mathbf{G}\to\mathbf{G}^{c}
and
\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}}\to(\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}})^{c}
are isomorphisms.

Proof.

The first claim is
[
21
, Lemma 4.7]
. The second claim then follows from the first by embedding an arbitrary Hodge type datum
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
into one of Siegel type
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})
, where it can be explicitly checked that
\mathbf{Z}_{1,\mathrm{ac}}
is trivial. For the claim concerning
\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}}
, we observe that the map
\delta\colon\mathbf{G}\to\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}}
restricts to a surjection
\delta\colon\mathbf{Z}\to\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}}
(e.g., see
[
37
, Example 19.25]
). From here, it’s easy to check that
\delta
surjects
\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{ac}}
onto
\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{ab}}_{\mathrm{ac}}
, and the claim follows.
∎

We set
\mathcal{G}^{c}
to be the parahoric model of
\mathbf{G}
induced by
\mathcal{G}
in the sense of §
2.1.5
. By construction, we see that
G\to G^{c}
extends to a morphism of group
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-schemes
\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}^{c}
. For a conjugacy class of cocharacters
\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt
of
G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
, we denote by
\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}^{c}
the image of this conjugacy class under
G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\to G^{c}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
.

4.1.2.
The shtuka over
\mathrm{Sh}_{\sf K}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E}
.

We now recall the existence of a “universal shtuka” which lives over the Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{\sf K}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E}
, as in
[
42
, §4.1]
and
[
7
, §4.2]
.

For a normal compact open subgroup
{\sf K}_{p}^{\prime}\subseteq{\sf K}_{p}
, we temporarily write
{\sf K}^{\prime}
for the product
{\sf K}_{p}^{\prime}{\sf K}^{p}
. Then the transition morphism

(4.1)

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\mathsf{K}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\sf K}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E}

is a finite étale Galois cover with Galois group
\mathsf{K}_{p}/(\mathsf{K}_{p}^{\prime}\mathbf{Z}(\mathbb{\mathbb{Q}})^{-}_{\mathsf{K}})
, where
\mathbf{Z}(\mathbb{\mathbb{Q}})^{-}_{\mathsf{K}}
is the closure of
\mathbf{Z}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathsf{K}
in
\mathsf{K}
(see
[
28
, §1.5.8]
). We consider the infinite-level Shimura variety

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E}\vcentcolon=\varprojlim_{{\sf K}_{p}^{\prime}\subseteq{\sf K}_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}_{p}^{\prime}{\sf K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E},

which exists as an
E
-scheme as each
\pi_{\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\mathsf{K}}
is affine (see
[
47
,
Tag 01YX
]
), and forms a
\mathsf{K}_{p}/\mathbf{Z}(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathsf{K}}^{-}
-torsor for the pro-etale site (in the sense of
[
5
]
) on
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E}
.

The map
\mathsf{K}_{p}\to\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
factorizes through
\mathsf{K}_{p}/\mathbf{Z}(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathsf{K}}^{-}
(see
[
28
, §1.5.8]
). Thus, as in
[
7
, §4.2]
or
[
21
, §2.1.5 and §4.3]
, from the
\mathsf{K}_{p}/\mathbf{Z}(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathsf{K}}^{-}
-torsor

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}^{p}}({\mathbf{G}},X)_{E}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}_{p}{\sf K}^{p}}({\mathbf{G}},X)_{E},

one constructs a
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
torsor
\mathbb{P}_{\sf K}
on the pro-étale site (see
[
45
]
) of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\sf K}({\mathbf{G}},X)^{\textup{an}}_{E}
.

Proposition 4.2

(
[
42
, Proposition 4.1.4]
and
[
7
, Proposition 4.4]
)
.

There exists a
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{{\sf K},E}
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{\sf K}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E}^{\lozenge}\to\textup{Spd}(E)
with one leg bounded by
\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}_{h}^{c}
which is associated to
\mathbb{P}_{\sf K}
in the sense of
[
42
, §2.5]
. Moreover, if
\mathsf{K}^{\prime}=\mathsf{K}_{p}{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\prime}
and
g
is in
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is such that
g^{-1}\mathsf{K}^{p}g\subseteq{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\prime}
, there exists compatible isomorphisms

(4.2)

t_{\mathsf{K},\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}(g)^{\ast}(\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{\prime},E})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K},E}.

4.1.3.
Integral local Shimura varieties

Let us define (see
[
49
, Definition 24.1.1]
), a
parahoric local Shimura datum
to be a triple
(\mathcal{G},b,\mu)
where

•

\mathcal{G}
is a parahoric group
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme with generic fiber
G
,

•

\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt
is a conjugacy class of minuscule cocharacters of
G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
,

•

and
b
is an element of
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
inducing an element of
B(G,\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}^{-1})
(see
[
44
, Definition 2.3]
).

The reflex field of
(\mathcal{G},b,\mu)
, usually denoted
E
, is the field of definition of
\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt
. A morphism
f\colon(\mathcal{G}_{1},b_{1},\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}_{1})\to(\mathcal{G},b,\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{})
of parahoric local Shimura datum is is a morphism of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-group schemes
f\colon\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}
carrying
\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}_{1}
to
\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt
and
b_{1}
to
b
. If such a morphism exists, then
E_{1}\supseteq E
. We say that
f
is an
ad-isomorphism
if
f\colon G_{1}\to G
is an ad-isomorphism.

Given a parahoric local Shimura datum
(\mathcal{G},b,\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{})
with reflex field
E
, we obtain a presheaf

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{G},b,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}}}^{\mathrm{int}}\colon\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{O}_{\breve{E}}}\to\mathbf{Set}

assigning to any perfectoid space
S
in characteristic
p
the set of isomorphism classes of tuples
(S^{\sharp},\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}},i_{r})
, where:

•

S^{\sharp}
is the untilt of
S
over
\mathcal{O}_{\breve{E}}
associated with
S\to\textup{Spd}(\mathcal{O}_{\breve{E}})
,

•

(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
is a
\mathcal{G}
-shtuka on
S
with one leg along
S^{\sharp}
which is bounded by
\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt
(see
[
42
, Definition 2.4.3]
),

•

and
i_{r}
is an isomorphism of
\mathcal{G}
-torsors
\mathcal{G}\big|_{\mathcal{Y}_{[r,\infty)}(S)}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathscr{P}\big|_{\mathcal{Y}_{[r,\infty)}(S)}
for large enough
r
, under which
\phi_{\mathscr{P}}
is identified with
b\times\mathrm{Frob}_{S}
(see
[
49
, §25.1]
).

By
[
49
, §25.1]
and
[
13
, Proposition 2.23]
, the presheaf
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{G},b,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}}}^{\mathrm{int}}
is a small
v
-sheaf (in the sense of
[
46
, Definition 12.1]
), which is called the integral local Shimura variety associated to the local Shimura datum
(\mathcal{G},b,\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{})
.

If
k_{E}
denotes the residue field of
E
, then by
[
14
, Proposition 2.30]
, we have a natural identification

(4.3)

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{G},b,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}}}^{\mathrm{int}}(\textup{Spd}(\bar{k}_{E}))=X_{\mathcal{G}}(b,\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}^{-1})(\bar{k}_{E}),

where
X_{\mathcal{G}}(b,\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}^{-1})
denotes the affine Deligne-Lusztig variety associated to the triple
(\mathcal{G},b,\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}^{-1})
(see
[
42
, Definition 3.3.1]
). For any point
x
of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G},b,\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}}(\textup{Spd}(\bar{k}_{E}))
we write
(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G},b,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}}})^{\wedge}_{/x}
for the formal completion of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{G},b,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}}}^{\mathrm{int}}
at
x
in the sense of
[
14
]
.

4.1.4.
The conjecture of Pappas and Rapoport

We now state the Pappas–Rapoport conjecture. For details, see
[
42
, §4.2]
and
[
7
, §4.3]
.

Suppose we have a normal flat
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-model
\mathscr{S}_{\sf K}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\sf K}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E}
, equipped with an extension
\mathscr{P}_{\sf K}
of the
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{{\sf K},E}
to a
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka defined over
\mathscr{S}_{\sf K}^{\lozenge/}
. Note that
\mathscr{P}_{\sf K}
is necessarily bounded by
\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}_{h}^{c}
by
[
7
, Lemma 2.1]
.

For any point
x
of
\mathscr{S}_{\sf K}(\bar{k}_{E})
, the pullback
x^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\sf K}
defines a
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka over
\textup{Spd}(\bar{k}_{E})
. In turn, by
[
42
, Example 2.4.9]
,
x^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\sf K}
determines a pair
(\mathcal{P}_{x},\phi_{x})
consisting of a
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-torsor
\mathcal{P}_{x}
over
\textup{Spec}\,(W(\bar{k}_{E}))
along with an isomorphism

\displaystyle\phi_{x}:\phi^{\ast}(\mathcal{P})[1/p]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{P}_{x}[1/p].

Here
\phi
denotes the Frobenius homomorphism for
W(\bar{k}_{E})
. After choosing a trivialization of
\mathcal{P}_{x}
, we obtain an element
b_{x}
in
G^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, and changing the trivialization will change
b_{x}
to some
\sigma
-conjugate of
b_{x}
. Therefore we obtain a well-defined conjugacy class
[b_{x}]
in
B(G^{c})
.

Since the shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\sf K}
is bounded by
\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}_{h}^{c}
, the same holds for
(\mathcal{P}_{x},\phi_{x})
, so the pair
(\mathcal{P}_{x},\textup{id})
determines a point
x_{0}
of
X_{\mathcal{G}}^{c}(b_{x},(\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}^{c}_{h})^{-1})(\bar{k}_{E})
. Hence
X_{\mathcal{G}}(b_{x},\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}_{h}^{-1})(\bar{k}_{E})
is nonempty, so by
[
18
, Theorem A]
, it follows that
[b_{x}]
belongs to
B(G^{c},(\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}_{h}^{c})^{-1})
. Then the triple
(\mathcal{G}^{c},b_{x},\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}_{h}^{c})
defines a parahoric local Shimura datum, and we can consider the integral local Shimura variety
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},b_{x},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}}_{h}^{c}}
along with the base point
x_{0}
(which makes sense by (
4.3
)).

Definition 4.3

(
[
42
, Conjecture 4.2.2]
and
[
7
, Conjecture 4.5]
)
.

Consider a system
\{\mathscr{S}_{{\sf K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
of normal flat
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-models
\mathscr{S}_{\sf K}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\sf K}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
for
{\sf K}={\sf K}_{p}{\sf K}^{p}
with
{\sf K}^{p}
varying over all sufficiently small compact open subgroups of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.

We say the system
\{\mathscr{S}_{\sf K}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
is an
canonical integral model for

\{\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
if the following properties are satisfied.

(1)

For every discrete valuation ring
R
of characteristic
(0,p)
over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
,

(4.4)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})(R[1/p])=\left(\varprojlim_{{\sf K}^{p}}\mathscr{S}_{\sf K}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\right)(R).

(2)

For every sufficiently small
{\sf K}^{p}\subseteq{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
{{\sf K}^{\prime}}^{p}\subseteq{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and element
g
of
{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
with
g^{-1}{{\sf K}}^{p}g\subseteq{\sf K}^{p}
, there are finite étale morphisms

t_{\mathsf{K},\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}(g)\colon\mathscr{S}_{{\sf K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\to\mathscr{S}_{{\sf K}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})

extending the corresponding maps on the generic fiber.

(3)

The
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{{\sf K},E}
extends to a
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\sf K}
on
\mathscr{S}_{\sf K}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})^{\lozenge/}
for every sufficiently small
{\sf K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.

(4)

Consider
x
in
\mathscr{S}_{\sf K}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})(\bar{k}_{E})
with corresponding element
b_{x}
of
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, and let
x_{0}
be the natural base point in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},b_{x},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}}_{h}^{c}}^{\mathrm{int}}(\bar{k}_{E})
. Then there is an isomorphism of
v
-sheaves

(4.5)

\displaystyle\Theta_{x}:\left(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},b_{x},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}_{h}^{c}}}\right)^{\wedge}_{/x_{0}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\left(\mathscr{S}_{\sf K}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})^{\wedge}_{/x}\right)^{\lozenge},

such that
\Theta_{x}^{\ast}(\mathscr{P}_{\sf K})
agrees with the universal
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{univ}}
on
