\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},b_{x},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}_{h}^{c}}}^{\mathrm{int}}
.

We observe the following uniqueness properties concerning a system of models satisfying the Pappas–Rapoport conjecture.

Proposition 4.4

(cf.
[
42
, Corollary 2.7.10 and Theorem 4.2.4]
)
.

An canonical integral model of
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
and the
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtukas
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}}
on these canonical integral models, are unique up to unique isomorphism
(
if they exist
)
.

The following is then what we refer to as the
Pappas–Rapoport conjecture
.

Conjecture 4.5

(Pappas-Rapoport)
.

For any parahoric Shimura datum
(\mathcal{G},\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
, there exists an canonical integral model for
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{\sf K}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
.

Conjecture
4.5
is known in the following cases, where we have elected to use our notation from §
3.3
for maximal clarity.

Theorem 4.6

(
[
42
, Theorem 4.5.2]
and
[
10
, Corollary 4.1.5]
)
.

For any parahoric Shimura datum
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
of Hodge type, the system
\{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
satisfies the conditions of the Pappas–Rapoport conjecture.

Remark 4.7
.

In
[
42
]
, Pappas and Rapoport prove the conjecture under the stubborn technical assumption that
{\sf K}_{p}
is a
stabilizer
parahoric, i.e., where
{\sf K}_{p}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
for a parahoric group scheme
\mathcal{G}
which is also the Bruhat–Tits stabilizer group scheme of a point in the extended Bruhat–Tits building of
G
. This assumption frequently holds; for example every parahoric
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-model of
G
is of this form if
\pi_{1}(G)_{I}
is torsion free. That says, it eliminates many examples of Shimura varieties of abelian type (e.g., many cases of type
D{H}
). The theorem was subsequently extended to arbitrary Shimura data of Hodge type in
[
10
]
.

Theorem 4.8

(
[
7
, Theorem A]
)
.

If
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is a parahoric Shimura datum of toral type, then
\{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
satisfies the conditions of the Pappas–Rapoport conjecture.

Proof.

This follows from
[
7
, Theorem A]
along with Proposition
3.22
.
∎

Theorem 4.9

(
[
21
, Proposition 5.35]
)
.

If
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is a parahoric Shimura datum of abelian type where
\mathcal{G}
is reductive, then the canonical integral models
\{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}}^{{\mathrm{Kis}}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
from
[
22
]
satisfy the conditions of the Pappas–Rapoport conjecture.

4.2.
Statement of the main result and applications

We now state our main result and give some applications to the study of Kisin–Pappas–Zhou models.

Theorem 4.10
.

Let
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
be an acceptable parahoric Shimura data of abelian type, and let
\mathfrak{d}
be a well-adapted very good parahoric Shimura datum of Hodge type. Then, the system
\{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
satisfies the conditions of the Pappas–Rapoport conjecture.

As an immediate implication, and as an opportunity to reemphasize the mildness of the acceptability condition, we record the following corollary.

Corollary 4.11
.

Suppose that
p>3
. Then, the Pappas–Rapoport conjecture holds for all parahoric Shimura data of abelian type.

In addition, utilizing Proposition
4.4
, and the previously known cases of the Pappas–Rapoport conjecture stated above, we can deduce the following omnibus independence result where, again, we are using notation from §
3.3
.

Corollary 4.12
.

Suppose that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is an acceptable parahoric Shimura datum of abelian type. Then, the following statements are true.

(1)

The system
\{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
is canonically independent of the choice of
\mathfrak{d}
.

(2)

If
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is of Hodge type, then the system
\{\mathscr{S}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
is canonically isomorphic to the system
\{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
for any
\mathfrak{d}
.

(3)

If
\mathcal{G}
is reductive then the system
\{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}}^{{\mathrm{Kis}}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
is canonically isomorphic to the system
\{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
for any
\mathfrak{d}
.

Given this independence result, the notation
\{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
and
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
for any of the systems of models discussed in §
3.3
is unambiguous.

Remark 4.13
.

One implication of Corollary
4.12
is the fact that the more naively constructed models
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{{\varnothing}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
possess a local model diagram (e.g., see (3) of
[
29
, Theorem 5.2.12]
) even if
[
29
, Condition (5.1.11.1)]
does not hold. Indeed, this follows by combining
[
29
, Theorem 5.2.12]
and (2) of Corollary
4.12
.

Lastly, we observe that Theorem
4.10
implies functoriality for the systems
\{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
.

Theorem 4.14
.

Suppose that
\alpha\colon(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})\to(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is a morphism of acceptable parahoric Shimura data of abelian type. Then, there exists a unique morphism

\alpha\colon\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}},

which is equivariant for the map
\alpha\colon\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and whose generic fiber recovers the map

\alpha\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})_{E_{1}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E_{1}}.

Proof.

This follows from the arguments of
[
42
, Corollary 4.3.2]
, and
[
10
, Corollary 4.0.9]
. We replicate the proof here for completeness.

We first observe, as in
[
42
, Proof of Corollary 4.3.2]
, that the product

\mathscr{S}^{\prime\prime}\vcentcolon=\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})\times_{\textup{Spec}\,\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}

determines an integral model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{{\sf K}_{p,1}\times{\sf K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X}_{1}\times\mathbf{X})
which satisfies Conjecture
4.5
. Then by
[
42
, Theorem 4.3.1]
, the graph of
\alpha
determines a unique morphism of integral models
\mathscr{S}_{{\sf K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})\to\mathscr{S}^{\prime\prime}
which extends the graph of
\alpha
on the generic fiber. We obtain the desired morphism by composition with the projection
\mathscr{S}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathscr{S}_{{\sf K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E_{1}}
.
∎

Remark 4.15
.

We remark that Theorem
4.14
is also proved in
[
50
]
, by establishing a modified version of Conjecture
4.5
using
\mathcal{G}^{\textup{ad}}
in place of
\mathcal{G}^{c}
; see Remark
1.1
.

5.
The proof of the main result

This final section is devoted to the proof of our main theorem (Theorem
4.10
). This will require
the development of multiple ancillary concepts and results that we hope will be useful in other contexts. We fix notation as at the beginning of §
4
.

5.1.
An auxiliary Kisin–Pappas–Zhou model

A useful heuristic for Shimura data of abelian type is that they are ‘spanned’ by two (largely orthogonal) extremes: Shimura data of Hodge type and Shimura data of toral type. Finding a precise formalism to realize this heuristic, especially when their integral models are part of the picture, is somewhat subtle. That said, this was achieved at hyperspecial level by Lovering in
[
31
]
. The goal of this subsection is to extend this formalism to the Kisin–Pappas–Zhou models of §
3
.

5.1.1.
The Lovering construction

We begin by recalling the underlying Shimura datum as defined in
[
31
, §4.6]
. Let us fix an extension
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
of
\mathbf{E}
. Define then the group

\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\vcentcolon=\mathbf{G}\times_{\delta,\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}},r}\mathbf{T}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}},

where
\mathbf{T}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{Res}}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}/\mathbb{Q}}\,\,\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}
,
\delta
is the canonical map
\mathbf{G}\to\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}}
, and
r
is obtained as the composition

{\mathrm{Res}}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}/\mathbb{Q}}\,\,\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{{\mathrm{Res}}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}/\mathbb{Q}}\,\,\mu_{h}}{\mathrm{Res}}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}/\mathbb{Q}}\,\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}^{\textup{ab}}\xrightarrow{N}\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}}.

Here
\mu_{h}\colon\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\to\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}
denotes the composition
\delta\circ\mu
for any element
\mu
of
\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}_{h}
, which is defined over
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
by the definition of reflex field and independent of choice since
\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}}_{\mathbb{C}}
is abelian, and
N
denotes the natural norm map. Observe that
\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}^{\textup{der}}=\mathbf{G}^{\textup{der}}
, so we have a short exact sequence

1\to\mathbf{G}^{\textup{der}}\to\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\to\mathbf{T}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\to 1.

Furthermore, we have an exact sequence

1\to\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\to\mathbf{G}\times\mathbf{T}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{(x,y)\mapsto\delta^{-1}(x)r(y)}\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}}\to 1,

and thus
\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}
is reductive (see
[
37
, Corollary 21.53]
).

Let
\{\tau\}
be the set of archimedean places of
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
, and let
\{[\tau]\}
denote the set of equivalences classes under the relation
\tau\sim\overline{\tau}
. Let
\tau_{0}\colon\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{C}
be the natural inclusion which defines an element
[\tau_{0}]
of
\{[\tau]\}
. Then, we have that
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\otimes{Q}\mathbb{R}
is isomorphic to
\prod_{[\tau]}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{[\tau]}
, where
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{[\tau]}
is
\mathbb{R}
or
\mathbb{C}
if
\tau
is real or complex, respectively. We define

h_{\mathbf{T}}\colon\mathbb{S}\to(\mathbf{T}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}){R}=\prod_{[\tau]}{\mathrm{Res}}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\tau}/\mathbb{R}}\,\,\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{[\tau]}},

to be the unique morphism having trivial projection
h_{\mathbf{T}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}},[\tau]}
to every
[\tau]
not equal to
[\tau_{0}]
, and satisfying

h_{\mathbf{T}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}},[\tau_{0}]}(z)=\begin{cases}z\overline{z}&\mbox{if}\quad\tau_{0}\text{ is real},\\
z&\mbox{if}\quad\tau_{0}\text{ is complex},\end{cases}

for
z
in
\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{R})=\mathbb{C}^{\times}
.

One may show (see
[
31
, Proposition 4.6.5]
) that for any element
h
of
\mathbf{X}
, the pair

(5.1)

(h,h_{\mathbf{T}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}})\colon\mathbb{S}\to\mathbf{G}_{\mathbb{R}}\times(\mathbf{T}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{R}},

factorizes through
(\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{R}}
, that the resulting
\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R})
-conjugacy class
\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}
is independent of the choice of
h
, and that the pair
(\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}},\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}})
is a Shimura datum with reflex field
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
. Moreover, there is a commutative diagram of Shimura data:

(5.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}},\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbf{T}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}},\{h_{\mathbf{T}}\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}},\mathbf{X}^{\textup{ab}}),}}

where
\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\to\mathbf{G}
,
\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\to\mathbf{T}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}
, and
\mathbf{G}\to\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}}
are the natural maps, and the map
\mathbf{T}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\to\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ab}}
is the norm map. We call
(\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}},\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}})
the
Lovering construction
applied to
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
. When
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=\mathbf{E}
, we simplify
\mathbf{T}_{\mathbf{E}}
,
\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{E}}
, and
\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{E}}
to
\mathbf{T}
,
\mathbf{B}
, and
\mathbf{Y}
, respectively.

We now record the following pleasant functoriality property of the Lovering construction.

Proposition 5.1

(
[
31
, Lemma 4.6.6]
)
.

Let
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})
be another Shimura datum and let
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
be a field containing the compositum
\mathbf{E}_{1}\mathbf{E}
. Suppose that
f\colon\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{der}}\to\mathbf{G}^{\textup{der}}
is an isogeny which induces an isomorphism
f^{\mathrm{ad}}\colon(\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{ad}},\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\textup{ad}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}(\mathbf{G}^{\textup{ad}},\mathbf{X}^{\textup{ad}})
. Then there exists a unique map
g\colon\mathbf{B}_{1,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\to\mathbf{B}
filling in the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{der}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{B}_{1,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{T}_{1,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{G}^{\textup{der}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}

where
N
is the natural norm map. Moreover,
g
induces a morphism of Shimura data
(\mathbf{B}_{1,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}},\mathbf{Y}_{1,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}})\to(\mathbf{B},\mathbf{Y})
.

5.1.2.
Relationship to Kisin–Pappas–Zhou models

We now explain how the Lovering construction fits into the theory of Kisin–Pappas–Zhou models.

To begin, let us fix a very good parahoric Shimura of Hodge type
\mathfrak{d}=(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
well-adapted to
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
, and let us explicitly denote by
f
the implicitly defined isogeny
\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{der}}\to\mathbf{G}^{\textup{der}}
. We further set
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}_{1}
.

Consider the Lovering constructions
(\mathbf{B},\mathbf{Y})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{1,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}},\mathbf{Y}_{1,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}})
. For notational simplicitly we shorten
(\mathbf{B}_{1,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}},\mathbf{Y}_{1,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}})
to
(\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}_{1}^{\prime})
, and similarly for other attendant notation. Observe that both of these Shimura data are of abelian type and, in fact,
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})
is an associated very good parahoric Shimura datum of Hodge type adapted to both. Indeed, this follows since

(5.3)

{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1}}^{\textup{der}}=\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{der}}\ \ \text{ and }\ \ \mathbf{B}^{\textup{der}}=\mathbf{G}^{\textup{der}},

and so we may consider the isogenies

\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{der}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}}{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1}}^{\textup{der}}\ \ \text{ and }\ \ \mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{der}}\xrightarrow{f}\mathbf{B}^{\textup{der}},

which produce the desired isomorphisms on adjoint Shimura data (cf. Proposition
2.4
).

We would like to upgrade this setup to the level of parahoric Shimura data. To this end, observe that by (
5.3
) and Lemma
2.5
one deduces that there are natural identifications

\mathscr{B}(B,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathscr{B}(G\times T,\mathbb{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}),\ \ \text{ and }\ \ \mathscr{B}(B_{1}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathscr{B}(G_{1}\times T_{1}^{\prime},\mathbb{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}).

Thus, we may associate parahoric models
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime}
to our choice of parahoric models
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}_{1}
, parahoric models, respectively. In fact, it follows from Proposition
2.15

\mathcal{B}^{\prime}=\mathcal{G}\times_{\mathcal{G}^{\textup{ab}}}\mathcal{T},\ \ \text{ and }\ \ \mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{G}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\textup{ab}}}\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\prime},

where
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\prime}
are the unique parahoric models of
T
and
T_{1}^{\prime}
, respectively.

Now, applying (
5.2
) and Proposition
5.1
one produces a diagram of Shimura data

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\prime},\{h_{\mathbf{T}_{1}}\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{ab}},\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\textup{ab}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbf{B},\mathbf{Y})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X}).}}

In particular, we obtain a diagram of
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
-schemes

(5.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{M}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\prime},\{h_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1}}\})_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{N}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}},\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}})_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{L}_{p}}(\mathbf{B},\mathbf{Y})_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}}}

each map equivariant for the natural map of groups. Here, we write
\mathsf{L}_{p}=\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}=\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
,
\mathsf{M}^{\prime}_{p,1}=\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and
\mathsf{N}_{p,1}=\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\textup{ab}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. We then have the following relationship between the Lovering construction and Kisin–Pappas–Zhou models.

Proposition 5.2
.

There exists a unique diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{M}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\prime},\{h_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1}}\})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{N}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}},\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{L}_{p}}(\mathbf{B},\mathbf{Y})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})}}

whose maps are equivariant for the appropriate maps of groups, and which models (
5.4
) pulled back to the completion of
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
at a prime above
v
.

Proof.

The existence of an equivariant map

{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{M}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1},\{h_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1}}\})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}\to{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{N}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}},\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}

modeling the map on the generic fiber follows from Proposition
3.22
, while the existence of the equivariant map

{\mathscr{S}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}\to{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{N}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}},\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}

modeling the map on the generic fiber follows from Proposition
3.25
. To construct the equivariant maps

{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}\to{\mathscr{S}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}},\ \text{ and }\ {\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}\to{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{M}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1},\{h_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1}}\})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}},

modeling the maps on the generic fiber, it suffices by Proposition
3.28
and Proposition
3.27
to construct a map

{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}\times\mathsf{M}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1}\times\{h_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1}}\})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}.

Because the map
\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1}\to\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1}
is an isomorphism on derived subgroups, we see from Proposition
2.4
that it is an ad-isomorphism, and so the claim follows from Proposition
3.24
. The fact that this constructed square commutes may be checked on the generic fiber, where it is true by construction.

The existence of an equivariant map

{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}\to{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{L}_{p}}(\mathbf{B},\mathbf{Y})}

follows from Proposition
3.24
as the induced map
{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1}}^{\textup{der}}\to\mathbf{B}^{\textup{der}}
is identified with the isogeny
f\colon\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{der}}\to\mathbf{G}
and so Proposition
2.4
applies. We similarly deduce the existence of a map

{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{L}_{p}}(\mathbf{B},\mathbf{Y})}\to{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})},

using the fact that
\mathbf{B}^{\textup{der}}\to\mathbf{G}^{\textup{der}}
is an isomorphism, along with Proposition
3.24
and Proposition
2.4
.
∎

5.2.
Construction of the
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka

In this section we establish the following theorem.

Theorem 5.3
.

There exists a unique
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}
on
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
bounded by
\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}_{h}^{c}
, which is compatible with the
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-action and which models
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},E}
.

By
[
42
, Corollary 2.7.10]
, there can be at most one shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{{\sf K}_{p}}
modeling
\mathscr{P}_{{\sf K}_{p},E}
. Moreover, such a shtuka is automatically bounded by
\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}_{h}^{c}
by
[
7
, Lemma 2.1]
. Thus, it suffices to construct a
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}
which is compatible with the
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-action and which models
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},E}
. In fact, it suffices to construct a model with
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-action of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},E^{\prime}}
for any discrete algebraic extension
E^{\prime}
of
E
. Indeed, by
[
42
, Corollary 2.7.10]
one may lift the descent datum of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},E^{\prime}}
relative to
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},E}
to an integral descent datum, which is effective by
[
49
, Proposition 19.5.3]
.

The proof of the existence of such a model will occupy the rest of this subsection, and will be carried out in several steps.

Step 1: Group Theory

We begin by establishing the following group-theoretic result which will be useful in our construction.

Proposition 5.4
.

There is a canonical identification

{\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime}}^{c}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{G}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\textup{ab}}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1}}^{c}.

Proof.

Let us begin by verifying that there is a canonical isomorphism

(5.5)

{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1}}^{c}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{G}_{1}\times_{\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{ab}}}{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1}}^{c}.

Note here that we are using Lemma
4.1
to identify
\mathbf{G}_{1}
and
\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}}
with
\mathbf{G}_{1}^{c}
and
(\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}})^{c}
, respectively.

Observe that since
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1}
is an ad-isomorphism, we may use Proposition
2.4
to deduce that

Z(\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime})=Z(\mathbf{G}_{1})\times_{\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\textup{ab}}}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\prime}.

We then employ the following simple lemma.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{T}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{T}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{T}_{3},}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

be a Cartesian diagram, where
\mathbf{T}_{i}
, for
i=1,2,3
, are multiplicative groups over
\mathbb{Q}
, and such that
\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\circ}\to\mathbf{T}_{3}^{\circ}
is surjective. Then,

\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{ac}}=(\mathbf{T}_{1})_{\mathrm{ac}}\times_{(\mathbf{T}_{2})_{\mathrm{ac}}}(\mathbf{T}_{3})_{\mathrm{ac}}.

Proof.

This follows easily from Proposition
2.14
, and the observation that for a containment
A\subseteq B
of multiplicative groups over some field
F
, one has that
A_{a}=(B_{a}\cap A)^{\circ}
and
A_{s}=(B_{s}\cap A)^{\circ}
, where the subscripts
a
and
s
denote the maximal anisotropic and split subtori, respectively. Namely, one applies this with
A=\mathbf{T}
, and
B=\mathbf{T}_{1}\times\mathbf{T}_{2}
.
∎

Using this, and the fact that fiber products of groups commute with quotients, we easily deduce the existence of an isomorphism as in (
5.5
). Note that
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{c}
is a parahoric group by definition. The same is true of
\mathcal{G}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\textup{ab}}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1}}^{c}
. Indeed, this follows from Proposition
2.15
, since
G_{1}^{\textup{der}}
is
R
-smooth (recall that we assume
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
is very good) and thus Proposition
2.11
applies, implying that
\ker(\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}^{\textup{ab}})
is
\mathcal{G}^{\textup{der}}
which is is smooth with connected fibers. To conclude, it now suffices to show that
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{c}
and
\mathcal{G}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\textup{ab}}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1}}^{c}
correspond to matching points under the bijection

\mathscr{B}({B^{\prime}_{1}}^{c},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathscr{B}({G}_{1}\times_{{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}}}{T_{1}^{\prime}}^{c},\mathbb{Q}_{p}),

coming from (
5.5
).

We have the following commutative diagram of equivariant bijections (utilizing a combination of Lemma
2.5
and Proposition
2.15
several times)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{B}({G}_{1}\times_{{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}}}{T_{1}^{\prime}}^{c})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{B}({G}_{1}\times{T_{1}^{\prime}}^{c},\mathbb{Q}_{p})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{B}({G}_{1}\times{T}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}_{p})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{B}({B_{1}^{\prime}}^{c},\mathbb{Q}_{p})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{B}({B}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbb{Q}_{p}).}}

Tracing through the definitions, we see that the claim follows as the points we consider ultimately can be induced from the same point
(x,\ast)
in the top-right corner, where
\mathcal{G}_{1}=\mathcal{G}_{x}^{\circ}
. Here we are implicitly using that the formation of the (reduced) Bruhat–Tits building commutes with products.
∎

Step 2: Pullback and Descent to the Fiber Product

Let us now recall the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{M}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\prime},\{h_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1}}\})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{N}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}},\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{L}_{p}}(\mathbf{B},\mathbf{Y})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

from Proposition
5.2
, with the arrows labeled for convenience, and where the arrow
e
is defined to make the lower triangle commute.

Utilizing Theorem
4.6
and Theorem
4.8
there exist a
\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant
\mathcal{G}_{1}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}
and a
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant
{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1}}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{M}^{\prime}_{p,1}}
on
{\mathscr{S}^{{\varnothing}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}
and
{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{M}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1},\{h_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1}}\})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}
, respectively. We then obtain a
\mathcal{G}_{1}
-shtuka and a
{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1}}^{c}
-shtuka on
{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{L}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}
, given by
a^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}
and
d^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{M}^{\prime}_{p,1}}
, respectively.

We claim that
a^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}
and
d^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{M}^{\prime}_{p,1}}
are both equivariant relative to
\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Indeed, this follows from the equivariance of these maps, as for an element
g
of
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
we have natural isomorphisms

[g]^{\ast}a^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}\simeq a^{\ast}([a(g)]^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}})\simeq a^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}},

using the
\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariance of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}
, and similarly in the case of
d^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{M}^{\prime}_{p,1}}
.

Our plan is now to use Corollary
2.17
to glue the shtukas
a^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{{\sf K}_{p,1}}
and
d^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{M}^{\prime}_{p,1}}
in order to obtain a
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime c}
-shtuka on
\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}_{1}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
modeling the
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1},E}
living over the generic fiber
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}_{1}^{\prime})_{E^{\prime}}
, see Proposition
4.2
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\delta
be the natural map
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\textup{ab}}}
, and let
N\colon{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1}}^{c}\to\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\textup{ab}}
be the
(
map induced by
)
the norm. Then there is a
\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant isomorphism of
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\textup{ab}}}
-torsors

\theta\colon\delta_{\ast}a^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}N_{\ast}d^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{M}_{p,1}}.

Proof.

To construct such an isomorphism, it suffices construct compatible isomorphisms over
\mathscr{S}_{{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{1}}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
, for each neat compact open subgroup
{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{1}}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Furthermore, by
[
42
, Corollary 2.7.10]
, it suffices to construct such compatible isomorphisms on
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{L}_{p,1}^{\prime}{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{1}}^{p}}(\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{E}
.

The shtukas
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1},E}
and
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{M}_{p,1}}
are functorially constructed from the corresponding étale realization functors
\mathbb{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1},E}
and
\mathbb{P}_{\mathsf{M}_{p,1}}
, respectively, in a way compatible with pullbacks along morphisms of Shimura varieties and pushforwards along morphisms of groups. Standard compatabilities of étale realizations (c.f.,
[
21
, Equation (4.3.2)]
) imply that there are natural isomorphisms

\delta_{\ast}a^{\ast}\mathbb{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}\simeq(b\circ a)^{\ast}\mathbb{P}_{\mathsf{N}_{p,1}}\simeq(c\circ d)^{\ast}\mathbb{P}_{\mathsf{N}_{p,1}}\simeq N_{\ast}d^{\ast}\mathbb{P}_{\mathsf{M}_{p,1}},

from where the claim follows. From this we deduce the existence of canonical isomormorphisms

\delta_{\ast}a^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}\simeq(b\circ a)^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{N}_{p,1}}\simeq(c\circ d)^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{N}_{p,1}}\simeq N_{\ast}d^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{M}_{p,1}},

where
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{N}_{p,1}}
is the unique model of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{N}_{p,1},E}
from Theorem
4.8
. The resulting composition
\theta:\delta_{\ast}a^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{{\sf K}_{p,1}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}N_{\ast}d^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{M}_{p,1}}
is the desired isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 5.7
.

There exists a
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}
on
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}_{1}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
which models
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1},E}
.

Proof.

Using Lemma
5.6
, we may apply Corollary
2.17
and Proposition
5.4
to deduce the construction of a
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}
on
{\mathscr{S}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}
. By construction this shtuka has an equivariant action of
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. As in the last paragraph, to show
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}
models the
{\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime}}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1},E}
, it suffices to show that there is an isomorphism

\mathbb{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1}}\times_{\theta}\mathbb{P}_{\mathsf{M}_{p,1}}\simeq\mathbb{P}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}.

Here we are applying Proposition
2.16
, which makes sense when one views these local systems as torsors as in
[
21
, §2.1.1]
. But, in view of Proposition
5.4
this is an elementary calculation (cf.
[
32
, §3.4.4]
).
∎

Step 3: Descending to
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}

We now show that there is a
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka on
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
which models
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
. As previously noted, this is sufficient to prove Theorem
5.3
. Let us begin by considering the
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka

\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\vcentcolon=\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1},\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}\times^{{\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime}}^{c}}\mathcal{G}^{c},

on
\mathscr{S}_{{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{1}}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}
.

Choose a connected component
{\mathscr{S}^{\prime}}^{+}
of
\mathscr{S}_{{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{1}}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}
, and let
\mathscr{S}^{+}
be the connected component of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}
containing its image under the map

(5.6)

\mathscr{S}_{{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{1}}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}.

Set
{\mathscr{P}^{\prime}}^{+}
to be the pullback of
\mathscr{P}^{+}
to
{\mathscr{S}^{\prime}}^{+}
.

Lemma 5.8
.

The
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
{\mathscr{P}^{\prime}}^{+}
descends uniquely to a
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}^{+}
on
\mathscr{S}^{+}
, which models the restriction of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
to
\mathscr{S}^{+}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}

Proof.

As noted in the proof of Proposition
3.24
, the map
{\mathscr{S}^{\prime}}^{+}\to\mathscr{S}^{+}
is a quotient by the group

\Delta=\ker(\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})^{\circ}\to\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}))/\ker(\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{1})^{\circ}\to\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}_{1})),

which acts through a finite quotient asn in
[
22
, §E.6]
. Thus, by
[
49
, Proposition 19.5.3]
, it suffices to show that there is a
\Delta
-action on
{\mathscr{P}^{\prime}}^{+}
which gives a descent datum. Arguing as in
Step 2
, it suffices to verify that there is a
\Delta
-descent datum on the
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local system
{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}^{+}
on
{\mathscr{S}^{\prime}}^{+}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
, whose definition is, mutatis mutandis, the same as that of
{\mathscr{P}^{\prime}}^{+}
. But, this is clear as the
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local system
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}_{1})_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
is the pullback of a
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local system on
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
by
[
21
, Equation (4.3.2)]
.

Thus we obtain a
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}^{+}
on
\mathscr{S}^{+}
. That this shtuka models the restriction of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
to
\mathscr{S}^{+}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
is clear by design. Indeed,
\mathscr{P}^{+}
is isomorphic to the
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka obtained as the descent of
{\mathscr{P}^{\prime}}^{+}
with respect to the
\Delta
-action which, as noted before, agrees with that of the restriction to
\mathscr{S}^{+}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
of the pullback of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
along the map in (
5.6
).
∎

Using the
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
-equivariant bijection

(5.7)

\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\pi_{0}({\mathscr{S}}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E\mathrm{ur}}}),

(see Lemma
3.20
and Lemma
3.21
) and
[
26
, Lemma 4.6.13]
, the group
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
acts transitively on
\pi_{0}(\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}})
. Thus, for any component
\mathscr{T}^{+}
of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}
we may choose some
g
in
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
such that

[g]\colon\mathscr{S}^{+}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathscr{T}^{+}.

We define

\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}},\mathscr{T}^{+}}\vcentcolon=[g]^{\ast}\mathscr{P}^{+}.

To see that this is canonically independent of the choice of
g
we again note by
[
42
, Corollary 2.7.10]
that it suffices to verify this on the generic fiber. There, the statement follows from the fact that
\mathscr{P}^{+}
models the restriction of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
to
\mathscr{S}^{+}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
, and the
\mathscr{A}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
-equivariance of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
. Moreover, by the same logic we see that
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}},\mathscr{T}^{+}}
models the restriction of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
to
\mathscr{T}^{+}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
.

For each neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and each connected component
\mathscr{T}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{+}
of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}
we may choose any connected component
\mathscr{T}^{+}
of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}
mapping to it. The fact here that the map

\pi_{0}(\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}})\to\pi_{0}(\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}})

is surjective follows from Lemma
3.20
.

Lemma 5.9
.

The shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{{\sf K}_{p},\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}},\mathscr{T}^{+}}
descends along the map
\mathscr{T}^{+}\to\mathscr{T}_{{\sf K}^{p}}^{+}
to a shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{{\sf K}^{p},\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}},\mathscr{T}^{+}_{{\sf K}^{p}}}
which models the restriction of
\mathscr{P}_{{\sf K}^{p},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
to
\mathscr{T}^{+}_{{\sf K}^{p},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
.

Proof.

The map
\mathscr{T}^{+}\to\mathscr{T}^{+}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is a profinite étale cover, and thus by
[
49
, Proposition 19.5.3]
to show that
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}},\mathscr{T}^{+}}
descends along this map, it suffices to produce descent data. Again by
[
42
, Corollary 2.7.10]
, this data can be obtained using the descent data for the restriction of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p,1},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
to
\mathscr{T}^{+}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
relative to the map
\mathscr{T}^{+}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}\to\mathscr{T}^{+}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
.

It is clear by the definition of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}_{p},\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}},\mathscr{T}^{+}}
,
[
22
, Lemma 2.2.5]
, and the equivariance of the map
\mathscr{T}^{+}\to\mathscr{T}^{+}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
that this descent is independent of all choices, and hence we obtain
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}},\mathscr{T}^{+}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
. We conclude by observing that
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}},\mathscr{T}^{+}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
models the restriction of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
to
\mathscr{T}^{+}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
since, by definition, they have the same descent data relative to the map
\mathscr{T}^{+}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}\to\mathscr{T}^{+}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
.
∎

Finally, we can construct the desired
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka on
\mathscr{S}_{{\sf K}_{p}{\sf K}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}
.

Construction 5.10
.

We set

\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}=\bigsqcup_{\mathscr{T}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{+}\in\pi_{0}(\mathscr{S}_{{\sf K}_{p}{\sf K}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}})}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}},\mathscr{T}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{+}}.

More precisely, let
k_{E}
denote the residue field of
E
. Then, as
\pi_{0}(\mathscr{S}_{{\sf K}_{p}{\sf K}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}})
is finite, the set of objects
S
of
\mathbf{Perf}_{k_{E}}
such that
S\to(\mathscr{S}_{{\sf K}_{p}{\sf K}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}})^{\lozenge/}
factorizes through some
(\mathscr{T}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{+})^{\lozenge/}
is a basis of
\mathbf{Perf}_{k_{E}}
, and on such an object we define the
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka over
S
to be that determined by the (unique) map
S\to(\mathscr{T}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{+})^{\lozenge/}
.

Proof of Theorem
5.3
.

It is clear from our definition of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}
that the collection
\{\mathscr{P}_{{\sf K}^{p},\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}\}_{{\sf K}^{p}}
forms a compatible family of
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtukas over
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}}}
. Also, by design,
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathcal{O}_{E^{u}r}}
models
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},E^{\mathrm{ur}}}
since it does so on each connected component. Theorem
5.3
follows.
∎

5.3.
The completion of the proof

We are now prepared to prove Theorem
4.10
. Given Theorem
3.12
and Theorem
5.3
it remains to verify the following proposition.

Proposition 5.11
.

For any neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and
x
in
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})(\overline{k}_{E})
, there exists an isomorphism of
v
-sheaves

\Theta_{x}\colon\left(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},b_{x},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}^{c}_{h}}}\right)^{\wedge}_{/x_{0}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\left(\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})^{\wedge}_{/x}\right)^{\lozenge},

such that
\Theta_{x}^{\ast}(\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})
is isomorphic to
\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{univ}}
, with notation as in Definition
4.3
.

The key to constructing such an isomorphism is the following result of Pappas–Rapoport.

Proposition 5.12

(
[
42
, Proposition 5.3.1]
)
.

Suppose that
f\colon(\mathcal{G}_{1},\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{}_{1},b_{1})\to(\mathcal{G},b,\mbox{$\raisebox{-2.54025pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.79993pt\mu\hskip-8.80005pt\raisebox{-4.2194pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-4.16pt\raisebox{3.7889pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 4.60007pt$}{})
is an ad-isomorphism of local Shimura data, and that
x_{1}
is an element of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}_{1},b_{1},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}_{1}}}(\overline{k}_{E_{1}})
with image
x
in
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G},b,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}}}(\overline{k}_{E})

(
note that
\overline{k}_{E_{1}}=\overline{k}_{E}
)
. Then the induced map

f\colon\left(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}_{1},b_{1},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}_{1}}}\right)^{\wedge}_{/x_{1}}\to\left(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G},b,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}}}\right)^{\wedge}_{/x}\times_{\textup{Spd}(\mathcal{O}_{E})}\textup{Spd}(\mathcal{O}_{E^{\mathrm{ur}}_{1}}),

is an isomorphism, and there is an identification

f^{\ast}(\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{univ}})\simeq\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}^{\mathrm{univ}}\times^{\mathcal{G}_{1}}\mathcal{G}.

Proof of Proposition
5.11
.

Consider the commutative diagram

(5.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{M}^{\prime}_{p,1}}(\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\prime},\{h_{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{1}}\})_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}}

such that

and

. We then obtain the diagram

from (

Let us now fix a point

of

Since the maps

are finite étale by Proposition

Here we use the obvious notation for the complete local rings of these Kisin–Pappas–Zhou models at these

2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}_{w}}}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1}}^{c},b_{(y,z)},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}_{(y,z)}}}

is a closed immersion. Because the map (
5.10
) is given at the level of objects by the push forward of shtukas along
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime c}\to\mathcal{G}_{1}\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1}}^{c}
, it follows from (
5.9
) that
\Theta_{w}^{\ast}(\mathscr{P}_{{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{1}}^{p}})
is isomorphic to
\mathscr{P}_{w}^{\mathrm{univ}}
.

On the other hand, since
{B}_{1}^{\prime c}\to{G}^{c}
is an ad-isomorphism, we deduce from Proposition
5.12
an isomorphism

\varepsilon\colon\Big(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime c},b_{w},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}_{w}}}\Big)^{\wedge}_{/w_{0}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\Big(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G},b_{x},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}_{x}}}\Big)^{\wedge}_{/x_{0}}.

We may then define

\Theta_{x}\vcentcolon=e\circ\Theta_{w}\circ\varepsilon^{-1}\colon\Big(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{G},b_{x},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.65}{$\displaystyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\textstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.77817pt}{$l$}\hskip-1.47495pt\mu\hskip-7.21117pt\raisebox{-2.95358pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-3.40889pt\raisebox{2.65222pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 3.7695pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-1.24472pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.88196pt\mu\hskip-4.31203pt\raisebox{-2.0675pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-2.03839pt\raisebox{1.85655pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 2.25403pt$}{}}$}}{\scalebox{.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mbox{$\raisebox{-0.88908pt}{$l$}\hskip-0.62997pt\mu\hskip-3.08002pt\raisebox{-1.47679pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}}\hskip-1.456pt\raisebox{1.32611pt}{$\color[rgb]{1,1,1}.$}\hskip 1.61002pt$}{}}$}}_{x}}}\Big)^{\wedge}_{/x_{0}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\textup{Spf }(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{x})^{\lozenge}.

That
\Theta_{x}(\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})
is isomorphic to
\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{x}
is easy as

\displaystyle\Theta_{x}(\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})

\displaystyle\simeq(\varepsilon^{-1})^{\ast}(\Theta_{w}^{\ast}(e^{\ast}\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})))

\displaystyle\simeq(\varepsilon^{-1})^{\ast}(\Theta_{w}^{\ast}(\mathscr{P}_{{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{1}}^{p}}\times^{\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime c}}\mathcal{G}^{c}))

\displaystyle\simeq(\varepsilon^{-1})^{\ast}(\Theta_{w}^{\ast}(\mathscr{P}_{{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}_{1}}^{p}})\times^{\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime c}}\mathcal{G}^{c})

\displaystyle\simeq(\varepsilon^{-1})^{\ast}(\mathscr{P}_{w}^{\mathrm{univ}}\times^{\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime c}}\mathcal{G}^{c})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{x}.

Here the first isomorphism holds by definition, the second holds by our construction of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
as in
Step 3
of §
5.2
, the third holds from abstract nonsense, the fourth holds by our construction of
\Theta_{w}
, and the final isomorphism holds from the properties of
\varepsilon
stated in Proposition
5.12
.
∎

The proof of Theorem
4.10
now follows by combining Theorem
3.12
with Theorem
5.3
and Proposition
5.11
.
